=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given an array of integers nums of size n and a positive integer threshold.
There is a graph consisting of n nodes with the i^th node having a value of nums[i]. Two nodes i and j in the graph are connected via an undirected edge if lcm(nums[i], nums[j]) <= threshold.
Return the number of connected components in this graph.
A connected component is a subgraph of a graph in which there exists a path between any two vertices, and no vertex of the subgraph shares an edge with a vertex outside of the subgraph.
The term lcm(a, b) denotes the least common multiple of a and b.
 
Example 1:

Input: nums = [2,4,8,3,9], threshold = 5
Output: 4
Explanation: 

 
The four connected components are (2, 4), (3), (8), (9).

Example 2:

Input: nums = [2,4,8,3,9,12], threshold = 10
Output: 2
Explanation: 

The two connected components are (2, 3, 4, 8, 9), and (12).

 
Constraints:

1 <= nums.length <= 10^5
1 <= nums[i] <= 10^9
All elements of nums are unique.
1 <= threshold <= 2 * 10^5

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def countComponents(self, nums: List[int], threshold: int) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

How to enumerate exact lcm pairs efficiently?

Given m factorization. Let divisors list D. For each x in D, we need all y in D such that lcm(x,y)=m. We can generate y as follows: For each prime p_i^e_i in m:
- If v_i(x) == e_i, then y's exponent can be 0..e_i.
- If v_i(x) < e_i, then y's exponent must be e_i.
So y = (m / x?) times arbitrary subset of prime powers where x already has full exponent? More concretely:
Let x = ∏ p_i^{a_i}, 0<=a_i<=e_i.
Let "deficient" primes D = {i: a_i < e_i}. For these, y must include p_i^{e_i}.
Let "full" primes F = {i: a_i = e_i}. For these, y can include p_i^{b_i}, 0<=b_i<=e_i.
Thus y = (∏_{i in D} p_i^{e_i}) * z, where z is any divisor of ∏_{i in F} p_i^{e_i}. Also y divides m. Need lcm(x,y)=m. Yes.

So for each x, number of y = ∏_{i in F} (e_i+1). Sum over x = ∏(2e+1). We can enumerate y by iterating over divisors z of full part. But doing for each x may duplicate pairs? Pseudo:
for m in 1..T:
    if m not present? We still need enumerate pairs only if at least two present? Could skip if no? But we can enumerate all m. Need factorization of m.
    factors = prime factorization of m (p, e)
    def dfs(idx, x, y):
        if idx == len(factors):
            if present[x] and present[y]: union(x,y) maybe if x!=y
            return
        p,e=factors[idx]
        # choose a,b in [0,e] with max=e
        pe = p**e
        # Cases:
        # a=e, b=0..e
        # b=e, a=0..e-1 (avoid double a=e,b=e)
        for b in range(e+1):
            dfs(idx+1, x*pe, y*(p**b))
        for a in range(e):
            dfs(idx+1, x*(p**a), y*pe)
This enumerates ordered pairs where for each prime max=e. But note for a=e,b=e included in first loop; second loop a<e. Good. Total choices (e+1)+e = 2e+1. It builds x,y. Need p**b computed. Could precompute powers for each factor.

Pseudo:
for m in 1..T:
    factors = factorize(m) -> list[(p,e,pe)]
    # generate list divs_m = [(x, full_part)]
    divs_m = []
    def gen(idx, x, fp):
        if idx == k:
            divs_m.append((x, fp))
        else:
            p,e,pe = factors[idx]
            # exponent a from 0..e
            pow = 1
            for a in range(e+1):
                if a == e:
                    gen(idx+1, x*pow, fp*pe)
                else:
                    gen(idx+1, x*pow, fp)
                pow *= p
    gen(0,1,1)
    for x, fp in divs_m:

            if present[x] and present[y2] and x < y2: union...
But wait `divs[fp]` includes all divisors of fp, not just those with full exponents? fp is product of prime powers where x has full exponent. Any divisor z of fp has exponents 0..e_i for those primes. Then y = required * z. For primes where x lacks full exponent, required includes p^e; z has no p. So y has full exponent. For primes where x has full exponent, y has arbitrary exponent. Thus lcm(x,y)=m. Good.

But note `divs[fp]` includes z=1, y=required. This y is the "deficient part" product p^e for primes x lacks. lcm(x,y)=m? Example m=12=2^2*3. x=2 (a2=1<2,a3=0<1). full_part=1, required=12, z=1, y=12. lcm(2,12)=12 yes. Good.

```
def get_factors(x):
    factors=[]
    while x>1:
        p=spf[x]
        e=0; pe=1
        while x%p==0:
            x//=p; e+=1; pe*=p
        factors.append((p,e,pe))
    return factors
```
Since spf[1]=0.

Implementation details:

```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def countComponents(self, nums: List[int], threshold: int) -> int:
        T = threshold
        present = [False] * (T + 1)
        for v in nums:
            if v <= T:
                present[v] = True

        # DSU
        parent = [-1] * (T + 1)

        def find(a):
            while parent[a] >= 0:
                if parent[parent[a]] >= 0:
                    parent[a] = parent[parent[a]]
                a = parent[a]
            return a

        def union(a, b):
            ra = find(a)
            rb = find(b)
            if ra == rb:
                return False
            if parent[ra] > parent[rb]:  # ra smaller
                ra, rb = rb, ra
            parent[ra] += parent[rb]
            parent[rb] = ra
            return True

        ans = len(nums)

        # divisors
        divs = [[] for _ in range(T + 1)]
        for d in range(1, T + 1):
            for m in range(d, T + 1, d):
                divs[m].append(d)

        # SPF
        spf = [0] * (T + 1)
        if T >= 1:
            # 1 has spf 0
            for i in range(2, T + 1):
                if spf[i] == 0:
                    spf[i] = i
                    if i * i <= T:
                        step = i
                        start = i * i
                        for j in range(start, T + 1, step):
                            if spf[j] == 0:
                                spf[j] = i
```
Wait this SPF sieve sets spf for primes >sqrt when i is prime and spf[i]==0, but for composite numbers whose smallest prime factor >sqrt? impossible. For composite j, spf[j] set by smallest prime. For prime i, spf[i]=i. For i composite, spf[i]!=0, skip. But inner loop for prime i sets spf for multiples starting i*i; for multiples less than i*i, they have smaller prime factor already set. Good. However for i=2, set spf[4,6,8,...] to2. For i=3, set spf[9,12,15,...] to3 if not set; 6 already set. Good. For i=5, set 25,30,...; 10 set by2. Good. This works. Complexity okay.

But note for i prime > sqrt(T), no inner loop. spf[i]=i. Good.

Could use linear sieve simpler:
```
spf = [0]*(T+1)
primes=[]
for i in range(2,T+1):
    if spf[i]==0:
        spf[i]=i; primes.append(i)
    for p in primes:
        if p > spf[i] or i*p > T: break
        spf[i*p]=p
```
This is O(T) and simple. Let's use linear sieve to avoid nested if? Both okay. Linear sieve with Python loops maybe okay for 2e5. Use:
```
spf = [0]*(T+1)
primes = []
for i in range(2, T+1):
    if spf[i] == 0:
        spf[i] = i
        primes.append(i)
    for p in primes:
        v = i * p
        if v > T or p > spf[i]:
            break
        spf[v] = p
```
This sets spf for all. For T=1, loop none. Good.

Factorize function:
```
        def factorize(x):
            factors = []
            while x > 1:
                p = spf[x]
                e = 0
                pe = 1
                while x % p == 0:
                    x //= p
                    e += 1
                    pe *= p
                factors.append((p, e, pe))
            return factors
```
For x=1 returns [].

Main loop:
```
        for m in range(1, T + 1):
            factors = factorize(m)
            divs_m = [(1, 1)]
            for p, e, pe in factors:
                new = []
                for x, fp in divs_m:
                    new.append((x, fp))  # a=0
                    mul = 1
                    for _ in range(1, e):
                        mul *= p
                        new.append((x * mul, fp))
                    mul *= p  # p^e
                    new.append((x * mul, fp * mul))
                divs_m = new

            for x, fp in divs_m:
                if not present[x]:
                    continue
                req = m // fp
                for y in divs[fp]:
                    y2 = req * y
                    if y2 > x and present[y2]:
                        if union(x, y2):
                            ans -= 1
        return ans
```

Need check `fp * mul` where mul after loop is p^e. For e=1: mul=1, range(1,1) none, mul*=p => p, append fp*p. Good. For e=2: range(1,2) one iteration: mul=1 -> mul*=p => p, append x*p, fp; after loop mul*=p => p^2, append x*p^2, fp*p^2. Good.
But note for a=0, we append (x, fp). For a=1..e-1, append (x*p^a, fp). For a=e, append (x*p^e, fp*p^e). Good.

Potential bug: In `new.append((x * mul, fp * mul))` for a=e, `mul` is p^e. But `fp` is product of prime powers for previous primes where x had full exponent. Multiplying by p^e is correct. Good.

Potential bug: `divs_m` order and duplicates? For each factor, each existing entry generates e+1 new entries. No duplicates because exponent vector unique. Good.

Potential bug: `divs[fp]` includes all divisors of fp. But if fp=0? no.

Potential bug: `present` array length T+1; `y2` could be 0? req>=1, y>=1, so no.

Potential bug: `ans` initial `len(nums)`. If nums contains value <=T but not connected, ans remains. If union reduces. If two values >T? no union. Good.

Let's think about optimizing inner loop with local variable bindings for speed:
```
parent = [-1]*(T+1)
def find(a, parent=parent): ...
```
In Python, nested functions with closure okay. For speed, we can inline find in union? Maybe okay. But 4.4M unions * find loops. Need optimize.

We can implement DSU find as iterative with path halving:
```
def find(a):
    while parent[a] >= 0:
        if parent[parent[a]] >= 0:
            parent[a] = parent[parent[a]]
        a = parent[a]
    return a
```
This is good.

Union:
```
def union(a,b):
    ra = find(a); rb = find(b)
    if ra == rb: return False
    if parent[ra] > parent[rb]:
        ra, rb = rb, ra
    parent[ra] += parent[rb]
    parent[rb] = ra
    return True
```
Need if parent[ra] > parent[rb] with negative sizes. Example ra size 2 parent=-2, rb size3 parent=-3. -2 > -3 true, swap so ra larger. Good.

Could use `nonlocal ans`? We don't need; in main loop, `if union(...): ans -= 1`.

```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def countComponents(self, nums: List[int], threshold: int) -> int:
        T = threshold
        present = [False] * (T + 1)
        cnt_le = 0
        for v in nums:
            if v <= T:
                present[v] = True
                cnt_le += 1

        if cnt_le == 0:
            return len(nums)
        if present[1]:
            return 1 + (len(nums) - cnt_le)

        parent = [-1] * (T + 1)

        def find(a):
            while parent[a] >= 0:
                if parent[parent[a]] >= 0:
                    parent[a] = parent[parent[a]]
                a = parent[a]
            return a

        def union(a, b):
            ra = find(a)
            rb = find(b)
            if ra == rb:
                return False
            if parent[ra] > parent[rb]:
                ra, rb = rb, ra
            parent[ra] += parent[rb]
            parent[rb] = ra
            return True

        divs = [[] for _ in range(T + 1)]
        for d in range(1, T + 1):
            for m in range(d, T + 1, d):
                divs[m].append(d)

        spf = [0] * (T + 1)
        primes = []
        for i in range(2, T + 1):
            if spf[i] == 0:
                spf[i] = i
                primes.append(i)
            for p in primes:
                v = i * p
                if v > T or p > spf[i]:
                    break
                spf[v] = p

        def factorize(x):
            factors = []
            while x > 1:
                p = spf[x]
                e = 0
                pe = 1
                while x % p == 0:
                    x //= p
                    e += 1
                    pe *= p
                factors.append((p, e, pe))
            return factors

        ans = len(nums)

        for m in range(1, T + 1):
            factors = factorize(m)
            divs_m = [(1, 1)]
            for p, e, pe in factors:
                new = []
                for x, fp in divs_m:
                    new.append((x, fp))
                    mul = 1
                    for _ in range(1, e):
                        mul *= p
                        new.append((x * mul, fp))
                    mul *= p
                    new.append((x * mul, fp * mul))
                divs_m = new

            for x, fp in divs_m:
                if not present[x]:
                    continue
                req = m // fp
                for y in divs[fp]:
                    y2 = req * y
                    if y2 > x and present[y2]:
                        if union(x, y2):
                            ans -= 1

        return ans
```

Need check `present[1]` when T>=1; threshold>=1, so index1 valid. Good.

Potential issue: If cnt_le>0 and present[1] false, but T=1? Then cnt_le could be1 if nums contains1, but present[1] true, early return. If nums contains no1 and no <=T? cnt_le=0. So no proceed with T=1 and present[1] false. Good.

Potential issue: `spf` linear sieve for T=1 if proceed? not possible. For T=2, okay.

Potential issue: `factorize(m)` for m=1 returns [] because while x>1 false. Good.

Potential issue: In `divs_m` generation, for e=1, `mul *= p` after loop -> p, append. Good.

Potential issue: `new.append((x * mul, fp * mul))` for a=e; if e=1, fp*mul = fp*p. Good.

Potential issue: `divs[fp]` precomputed; fp can be 0? no.

Potential issue: `union` uses parent list; if x or y2 >T? y2<=m<=T. Good.

Potential issue: If nums contains value 1 and threshold=1, early return `1 + (len(nums)-cnt_le)`. If nums=[1], cnt_le=1, return1. Good. If nums=[1,2], cnt_le=1 (2>T), return1+1=2. Graph no edge. Good.

Potential issue: If nums contains value 1 and threshold=2, nums=[1,2,3], cnt_le=2, return1+1=2. Graph 1-2 edge, 3 isolated. Good.

Potential issue: If nums contains value 1 and threshold=200000, all <=T connected. Good.

from typing import List

class Solution:
    def countComponents(self, nums: List[int], threshold: int) -> int:
        T = threshold
        present = [False] * (T + 1)
        cnt_le = 0

        for v in nums:
            if v <= T:
                present[v] = True
                cnt_le += 1

        if cnt_le == 0:
            return len(nums)

        # If 1 is present, it connects to every value <= threshold.
        if present[1]:
            return 1 + (len(nums) - cnt_le)

        parent = [-1] * (T + 1)

        def find(a):
            while parent[a] >= 0:
                if parent[parent[a]] >= 0:
                    parent[a] = parent[parent[a]]
                a = parent[a]
            return a

        def union(a, b):
            ra = find(a)
            rb = find(b)
            if ra == rb:
                return False
            if parent[ra] > parent[rb]:
                ra, rb = rb, ra
            parent[ra] += parent[rb]
            parent[rb] = ra
            return True

        # Precompute divisors for all numbers up to T.
        divs = [[] for _ in range(T + 1)]
        for d in range(1, T + 1):
            for m in range(d, T + 1, d):
                divs[m].append(d)

        # Smallest prime factor sieve.
        spf = [0] * (T + 1)
        primes = []
        for i in range(2, T + 1):
            if spf[i] == 0:
                spf[i] = i
                primes.append(i)
            for p in primes:
                v = i * p
                if v > T or p > spf[i]:
                    break
                spf[v] = p

        def factorize(x):
            factors = []
            while x > 1:
                p = spf[x]
                e = 0
                pe = 1
                while x % p == 0:
                    x //= p
                    e += 1
                    pe *= p
                factors.append((p, e, pe))
            return factors

        ans = len(nums)

        # Enumerate every exact lcm m <= T.
        for m in range(1, T + 1):
            factors = factorize(m)

            # divs_m stores (divisor, full_part), where full_part is the
            # product of prime powers p^e of m that fully divide divisor.
            divs_m = [(1, 1)]
            for p, e, pe in factors:
                new = []
                for x, fp in divs_m:
                    # exponent 0
                    new.append((x, fp))

                    # exponents 1 .. e-1
                    mul = 1
                    for _ in range(1, e):
                        mul *= p
                        new.append((x * mul, fp))

                    # exponent e
                    mul *= p
                    new.append((x * mul, fp * mul))
                divs_m = new

            for x, fp in divs_m:
                if not present[x]:
                    continue

                req = m // fp
                for y in divs[fp]:
                    y2 = req * y
                    if y2 > x and present[y2]:
                        if union(x, y2):
                            ans -= 1

        return ans
```
